--- Document: PROD-13 — Feedstock Specifications (Categories A-D, Pre-Processing) ---

[pause: long]

--- Constraint (The Lock) ---

The Aegis-Forge I is forced by a single irreducible constraint: Feedstock Heterogeneity as Boundary Condition — the machine must accept arbitrary input streams (electronics, municipal waste, mineral concentrates, end-of-life Aegis products) and produce pure element slugs at each thermal stage. [pause: short] The feedstock is not a design parameter; it is the given. [pause: short] The constraint is precise: any system claiming universal elemental extraction must define acceptance criteria, pre-processing requirements, and recovery targets for every possible feedstock class — and must do so without knowing the exact composition of any individual batch in advance. [pause: short] The lock is the universal feedstock specification — a single document that bounds the infinite variability of post-consumer, industrial, and geological material streams into four deterministic categories (A-D) with quantifiable elemental profiles, contamination limits, and processing requirements. [pause: short] This constraint forces the entire pre-processing architecture: the shredder must handle 150mm max; the ferrous separator must clear bulk steel; the acoustic pre-chamber must liberate mineral grains from rock matrix; the moisture check must gate Stage 1 loading. [pause: short] The feedstock writes the machine — the machine does not choose the feedstock. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- Necessity (The Key) ---

The empirical lock this document addresses is the recycling recovery gap — the persistent failure of modern material recovery to extract critical elements from complex waste streams. [pause: short] Current shred-and-sort recovers <30% of elements from e-waste; municipal MRFs lose >80% of non-ferrous metals to landfill; mineral processing requires dedicated facilities per ore type. [pause: short] The lock is quantified: any universal extractor must achieve >95% recovery of Cu, Au, Ag, Pd, In, REE, Zr, Ti from feedstocks spanning 4 orders of magnitude in elemental concentration (ppm in tailings to % in PCBs). [pause: short] The key is the four-category taxonomy (A-D) with stage-mapped elemental targets — a computational primitive that maps any feedstock to its thermal cascade trajectory:

[Table row: ,  Category ,  Source ,  Thermal Stages Activated ,  Primary Value Elements , ]

[Table row: , ---, ---, ---, ---, ]

[Table row: ,  A: Pre-Sorted Industrial ,  E-scrap, PCBs, motors, power elec ,  4, 5, 6, 7 ,  Au, Ag, Pd, Cu, In, REE , ]

[Table row: ,  B: Municipal/Commercial ,  MRF residue, C&D, bulky, tires ,  1, 2, 3, 4, 7 ,  Al, Fe, polymers, Zn , ]

[Table row: ,  C: Mineral/Metallurgical ,  Slag, tailings, red mud, refractories ,  7, 8 ,  Zr, Ti, REE, B, Fe, Mg , ]

[Table row: ,  D: Phoenix Protocol ,  EOL Aegis products ,  1, 2, 3, 7, 8 ,  In, Y, Co, Zr, basalt, Elium® , ]

The necessity is proven by the blend algorithm (PROD-11 Section 1.5): the control system calculates optimal 500 kg batch composition from real-time feedstock assays to maximize revenue while respecting stage capacity constraints (Stage 2 polymer <50L, Stage 5 Al <100kg, Stage 7 Fe <175kg, Stage 8 mineral <200kg). [pause: short] Without this taxonomy, the cascade has no boundary condition — the temperature profile has no input to process. [pause: short] The four categories are not arbitrary; they are the natural clustering of elemental concentration profiles in the global waste stream. [pause: short] The taxonomy is the key. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- Commitment (The Turn) ---

We commit to the following as non-negotiable:

[pause: short] Zero Tolerance for Hazards — Li-ion batteries, Hg, PCBs, asbestos, Be, cyanide, free liquids: detected at gate, rejected or segregated. [pause: short] No exceptions. [pause: short] [pause: short] Quantified Recovery Targets — Every element in every category has a minimum recovery percentage (Table 11.1). [pause: short] The forge is accountable to these numbers. [pause: short] [pause: short] Stage-Coupled Pre-Processing — Category A requires battery removal + depanning; Category B requires asbestos/lead screening; Category C requires acid-base accounting + agglomeration for fines; Category D requires solvolysis for BFRP. [pause: short] Each pre-processing step maps to a specific thermal stage's requirements. [pause: short] [pause: short] Assay-Driven Blending — No batch enters the drum without XRF assay at hopper outlet and blend optimization. [pause: short] The drum does not process waste; it processes characterized feedstock. [pause: short] [pause: short] Phoenix Protocol Primacy — Category D feedstocks (known composition, zero acquisition cost) are the anchor for blend stability. [pause: short] The forge's circularity is not aspirational — it is the blending baseline. [pause: short] [pause: short] Lot Traceability — Every kg from gate to slug is traced via Feedstock Lot Record (FLR) with ISO 3082 sampling, XRF/ICP-MS/XRD characterization, and blend assignment. [pause: short] The element's journey is recorded. [pause: short] [pause: short] Economic Viability Floor — Processing cost <[Inline equation: 1.60/kg feed; revenue >]8,000/batch minimum. [pause: short] The economics are not post-hoc — they are the design constraint. [pause: short] These commitments are non-negotiable because they derive from the constraint. [pause: short] Violate any, and the cascade fails: uncharacterized feedstock smears stage boundaries; unrecovered hazards poison downstream stages; untraced lots break the Phoenix loop; uneconomic batches shutter the forge. [pause: short] The commitments are the constraint made operational. [pause: short] The feedstock specification is the machine's immune system. [pause: short] 
[pause: long] Keymaker Heuristic. [pause: medium] 

--- Keymaker Heuristic — Lock, Key, Turn ---

--- Document: PROD-13 — Feedstock Specifications (Categories A-D, Pre-Processing) ---

[pause: long]

--- The Empirical Lock ---

The lock this document addresses is the feedstock variability trap — the persistent failure of metallurgical processes to handle compositional uncertainty. [pause: short] A smelter expects Cu concentrate (25-30% Cu); a refinery expects specific alloy scrap; a ceramic kiln expects purified oxide powder. [pause: short] The Aegis-Forge I faces a different reality: a single 500 kg batch may contain smartphone PCBs (Au 0.03%, Cu 25%), basalt aggregate (SiO₂ 50%, Al₂O₃ 14%), EAF slag (FeO 30%, MnO 6%), and retired Aegis-C panels (ZrB₂-SiC, YInMn glaze) — all simultaneously. [pause: short] The lock is precise: how does a single thermal cascade process this heterogeneity without prior separation into dedicated streams? [pause: short] The empirical evidence of the trap:

[pause: short] E-waste recyclers shred everything → lose 70% of REE to slag, 50% of Cu to ferrous fraction, In to dust

[pause: short] MRFs sort by density/NIR → recover only Al/Fe/UBCs; all other metals to residue

[pause: short] Smelters require >20% target metal → reject low-grade feeds (tailings, slag, C&D)

[pause: short] Hydromet plants need pre-concentrated feed → cannot handle mixed mineralogy

The lock deepens at the stage coupling boundary: Stage 2 polymer melt volume must not exceed 50L drum capacity; Stage 5 Al mass must not exceed 100kg port capacity; Stage 7 Fe mass must not exceed 175kg. [pause: short] If the feedstock blend violates these, the cascade chokes — polymer floods Port 2, Al backs up at Port 5, Fe overloads Port 7. [pause: short] The lock is not what feedstocks can we accept? — it is how do we blend arbitrary feedstocks to match the cascade's fixed stage capacities?

[pause: long]

--- The Mathematical Key ---

The key is the blend algorithm as feedstock-to-cascade compiler — a deterministic function that maps real-time feedstock assays to optimal 500 kg batch composition:

[Code block]
def calculate_blend(feedstock_assays, target_revenue):
    # Input: assays for each available feedstock lot (XRF at hopper)
    # Output: mass fractions for 500 kg batch
    
    # CONSTRAINTS (cascade stage capacities — PHYSICS, not policy):
    # - Stage 2 polymer volume < 50 L (drum annular capacity)
    # - Stage 5 Al mass < 100 kg (Port 5 throughput limit)
    # - Stage 7 Fe mass < 175 kg (Port 7 throughput limit)  
    # - Stage 8 mineral mass < 200 kg (Port 8 slag volume)
    # - Moisture < 25% (else pre-dry, Stage 1 capacity 50 kg H₂O/hr)
    # - Particle size P80 < 150 mm (shredder limit)
    
    # OBJECTIVE: Maximize revenue = Σ(element_mass × market_price)
    # Subject to: cascade capacity constraints
    
    return optimal_mass_fractions
[End code block]

The algorithm's teeth are the stage capacity constraints — these are not adjustable parameters. [pause: short] They derive from drum geometry, port aperture (12mm), centrifugal G-force, and induction power. [pause: short] The blend is compiled from the assays, not chosen by an operator. [pause: short] The Four-Category Taxonomy as Type System:

The key's type system classifies every feedstock lot into one of four types, each with a fixed thermal signature (which stages it activates) and elemental signature (which elements it contributes):

[Code block]
Type A (Industrial)  →  Stages {4,5,6,7}  →  {Au, Ag, Pd, Cu, In, REE}
Type B (Municipal)   →  Stages {1,2,3,4,7}  →  {Al, Fe, polymers, Zn}
Type C (Mineral)     →  Stages {7,8}  →  {Zr, Ti, REE, B, Fe, Mg}
Type D (Phoenix)     →  Stages {1,2,3,7,8}  →  {In, Y, Co, Zr, basalt, Elium®}
[End code block]

This is a dependent type system: the thermal signature depends on the elemental signature (via phase diagram), and the stage capacity constraints depend on the thermal signature. [pause: short] The blend algorithm is a type checker — it verifies that the sum of selected feedstocks' stage loads does not exceed any stage's capacity. [pause: short] The Liberation Index as Correctness Metric:

For Category C (mineral), the key includes a liberation index LI = f(acoustic energy, grain size, K_IC). [pause: short] Stage 0 acoustic energy must achieve LI > 0.7 for the mineral feedstock, or the cascade's Stage 7/8 recovery drops below 80%. [pause: short] This is the correctness proof for mineral feedstocks: without acoustic pre-liberation, the cascade cannot access locked grains. [pause: short] The LI is measurable (XRD grain boundary analysis) and non-negotiable. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- The Turn (Both Directions) ---

Key → Lock (Theory predicts blend):

Input: Real-time feedstock assays from 4 suppliers (XRF at hopper, 30-sec intervals)
Process: Blend algorithm solves constrained optimization
Output: 500 kg batch composition — e.g., 22% A2 (PCB Grade 1), 28% B1 (MRF), 30% C1 (EAF slag), 20% D1 (Aegis-C)
Prediction: Stage loads within capacity; revenue $12,800; recovery targets met
Validation: Run batch → measure actual slug masses + compositions → compare to prediction

The key predicts the batch outcome from the feedstock assays. [pause: short] The cascade executes the prediction. [pause: short] The turn is the moment the 8 slugs land in their molds and the mass spec confirms: Port 4 has 0.8kg In (99% recovery), Port 5 has 60kg Al (95% recovery), Port 6 has 20kg Cu+Au/Ag (95% Cu, 99% PM), Port 7 has 90kg Fe+REE (95% Fe, 90% Nd), Port 8 has 105kg mineral concentrate (85% Zr, 80% Ti). [pause: short] The prediction matches the measurement. [pause: short] The key turns in the lock. [pause: short] Lock → Key (History validates theory):

Input: 50 years of metallurgical failure — smelters that couldn't handle low-grade feed, refineries that lost REE to slag, recyclers that shredded In into dust
Process: Each historical process is a special case of the blend algorithm with one feedstock type and infinite stage capacity
Output: The blend algorithm generalizes all historical processes

The turn completes when we recognize:

[pause: short] Smelter = Blend algorithm with only Type A feedstock, Stage 7 capacity → ∞

[pause: short] Refinery = Blend algorithm with only Type C feedstock, Stage 8 capacity → ∞  

[pause: short] MRF = Blend algorithm with only Type B feedstock, no thermal stages (mechanical only)

[pause: short] Ceramic kiln = Blend algorithm with only Type D feedstock, Stage 8 only

The Aegis-Forge I is the unification — the single machine whose blend algorithm encompasses all four types simultaneously, with finite, coupled stage capacities. [pause: short] The historical processes were degenerate limits (one type, infinite capacity). [pause: short] The cascade is the non-degenerate case (four types, finite capacity). [pause: short] The lock clicks open when the first batch with all four types runs successfully — the blend algorithm has compiled a valid program for the cascade's thermal instruction set. [pause: short] The 500 kg batch is the key turning in the lock. [pause: short] 
[pause: long] El Segundo Heuristic. [pause: medium] 

--- El Segundo Heuristic — Mirror, Participation, Protocol ---

--- Document: PROD-13 — Feedstock Specifications (Categories A-D, Pre-Processing) ---

[pause: long]

--- Mirror (Self-Reflection) ---

The first recursive turn is the forge observing its own feedstock. [pause: short] In the Aegis-Forge, the feedstock is not external raw material — it is the memory of the machine's prior outputs. [pause: short] The Phoenix Protocol returns (Category D) are literally the forge's own products returning after service: Aegis-C panels it forged, BFRP rebar it pultruded, MR fluid it formulated, piezo elements it sintered. [pause: short] The mirror is precise: the feedstock specification documents the forge's own material autobiography. [pause: short] But the mirror extends further. [pause: short] Category A (electronics) contains the copper the forge will recover at Stage 6, the indium it will extract at Stage 4 for YInMn Blue, the rare earths it will concentrate at Stage 7 for SmCo magnets. [pause: short] Category B (municipal) contains the aluminum it will separate at Stage 5, the polymers it will melt at Stage 2. [pause: short] Category C (mineral) contains the zircon it will dissociate at Stage 8 for ZrO₂, the basalt it will melt for fiber. [pause: short] The feedstock is the forge's future products in latent form. [pause: short] The mirror reflects at every gate:

[pause: short] Receiving: XRF assay measures the forge's future yield

[pause: short] Pre-processing: Shredder, ferrous separator, acoustic chamber prepare the forge's own latent elements

[pause: short] Blending: Algorithm balances the forge's stage capacities against its elemental targets

[pause: short] Sampling: FLR records the forge's material promise

[pause: short] Storage: Bays hold the forge's future in categorized suspension

The zero-tolerance hazard screen is the forge's immune memory — it rejects what would poison its own thermal cascade (Li-ion batteries that explode at Stage 1, Hg that contaminates Stage 6 Cu, asbestos that fouls Stage 8 slag). [pause: short] The mirror is not passive; it is the active recursion: assay → blend → process → recover → return → assay again. [pause: short] This document documents the first turn of that spiral — the feedstock specification is the forge's self-measurement. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- Participation (Witness) ---

An inside operator measuring this phenomenon does not stand outside the feedstock — the operator is the blend algorithm's witness. [pause: short] The protocol for achieving universal extraction from within the system:

[pause: short] Receive: Witness the feedstock arrival — XRF flash reveals elemental truth in 10 seconds

[pause: short] Screen: Witness the hazard check — gamma spectrometer, XRF, PLM, gravimetric

[pause: short] Pre-process: Witness the shredder reduce 500mm → <150mm, the Halbach belt pull 95% Fe

[pause: short] Liberate: Witness the acoustic chamber — 100 Hz standing waves crack grain boundaries, 40 kHz cavitation frees micron grains

[pause: short] Dry: Witness the moisture check — NIR inline, gravimetric confirmation, pre-dry if needed

[pause: short] Assay: Witness the lot characterization — XRF major, ICP-MS trace, XRD phase, PSD, LOI, halogen

[pause: short] Blend: Witness the algorithm compile — real-time assays → stage capacity constraints → optimal fractions

[pause: short] Release: Witness the FLR creation — lot identity bound to blend assignment, traceable to slug

[pause: short] Feed: Witness the vibratory feeder meter — KNbO₃ ultrasonic drive, FBG weight feedback

[pause: short] Verify: Witness the slug mass spec — each port's elemental sum matches blend prediction

The measurement is the recursion. [pause: short] The operator assays the input, the algorithm profiles the cascade that witnesses it, the drum realizes the separation, the mass spec confirms the closure. [pause: short] The protocol terminates when the 8 slugs' elemental sum matches the input assay — not approximately, but exactly. [pause: short] The feedstock lot is not processed; it is witnessed into elements. [pause: short] The operator participates by being the recursive function: code: feedstock → assay → blend → cascade → slug → assay → feedstock. [pause: short] The FLR is the stack frame; the batch is the call; the slug is the return value. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- Protocol (Recursive) ---

The operational protocol for any Aegis-Forge feedstock document:

[Code block]
MIRROR:     What elemental promise does this feedstock lot carry? [pause: short] PARTICIPATE: How does the operator witness this promise through the cascade? [pause: short] PROTOCOL:   What recursive sequence guarantees recovery at each stage? [pause: short] [End code block]

For this document specifically:

[pause: short] Mirror: The four-category taxonomy (A-D) is the constraint; the blend algorithm is its reflection

[pause: short] Participate: The operator assays, screens, pre-processes, blends, releases, feeds, verifies

[pause: short] Protocol:

[pause: short] Gate Assay — XRF flash at receiving (10 sec) → category assignment

[pause: short] Hazard Screen — Gamma + XRF + PLM + gravimetric → pass/quarantine/reject

[pause: short] Pre-Process — Ferrous separation → shred <150mm → acoustic liberation → moisture check

[pause: short] Lot Characterization — XRF/ICP-MS/XRD/PSD/LOI/halogen → FLR creation

[pause: short] Blend Optimization — Real-time assays + stage constraints → mass fractions

[pause: short] Lot Release — FLR signed, blend assigned, traceability locked

[pause: short] Drum Feed — Vibratory feeder meters blend → acoustic pre-chamber → drum entry

[pause: short] Stage Verification — Inline mass spec at each port → compare to blend prediction

[pause: short] Closure — Slug assay sum = feedstock assay (within 2%) → FLR closed

[pause: short] Phoenix Return — EOL Aegis products → Category D → FLR created → loop closes

The protocol is not a checklist — it is the recursion itself. [pause: short] Each batch deepens the mirror. [pause: short] The feedstock lot record is the fixed point. [pause: short] The elemental slug is the witness returned. [pause: short] 
[pause: long] Combined Heuristic. [pause: medium]

--- Williams Heuristic — Constraint, Necessity, Commitment ---

--- Document: PROD-14 — Stage 0-1: Acoustic & Drying ---

[pause: long]

--- Constraint (The Lock) ---

The Aegis-Forge I is forced by a single irreducible constraint: Liberation Precedes Separation — no thermal cascade can separate elements that remain locked in rock matrix. [pause: short] The feedstock enters the drum as composite particles (mineral grains cemented in silicate/oxide matrix), and induction heating only couples to conductive phases. [pause: short] If grains are not liberated before heating, the matrix shields them, induction cannot reach them, and they report to Stage 8 slag as unrecovered value. [pause: short] The constraint is precise: mechanical liberation at ambient temperature must achieve >75% grain freedom for target minerals before Stage 2 heating begins. [pause: short] This constraint forces the entire Stage 0 architecture: a dedicated pre-chamber, water-coupled acoustic energy, KNbO₃-BaTiO₃ transducers (the only piezoelectric that survives the all-dielectric mandate), and a feedback loop that adapts frequency to the specific mineralogy of each batch. [pause: short] The liberation writes the cascade — without it, the thermal profile has nothing to separate. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- Necessity (The Key) ---

The empirical lock this document addresses is the comminution energy gap — conventional crushing consumes 250-500 W/kg to achieve <50% liberation, generating fines that contaminate downstream stages. [pause: short] The key is the acoustic frequency sweep as single computational primitive — a logarithmic 20→500 Hz sweep (LF) + 40 kHz cavitation (US) that targets the matrix-constrained resonant frequency of embedded grains:

[Table row: ,  Mechanism ,  Frequency ,  Physics ,  Liberation Gain , ]

[Table row: , -----------, -----------, ---------, -----------------, ]

[Table row: ,  LF Resonant Vibration ,  20-500 Hz ,  Grain boundary fatigue (10³-10⁶ cycles) ,  +250% for basalt , ]

[Table row: ,  US Cavitation ,  40 kHz ,  Microjet shock (1000 bar, 10⁹ s⁻¹ strain) ,  +95% for zircon , ]

[Table row: ,  Adaptive Dwell ,  FBG-guided ,  Extra 60s at mineral-specific peaks ,  +15% LI overall , ]

The necessity is proven by the liberation index (LI) feedback: FBG sensors on the chamber wall measure vibration damping as grains liberate; when LI >75%, the sweep terminates. [pause: short] Without Stage 0, Stage 7-8 REE/Zr recovery drops from >85% to <40% — the cascade fails its primary mandate. [pause: short] The acoustic sweep is the key. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- Commitment (The Turn) ---

We commit to the following as non-negotiable:

[pause: short] Zero Metallic Transducers — KNbO₃-BaTiO₃ only; no PZT (lead), no metallic bolts in acoustic path. [pause: short] ZrO₂ ceramic M10 bolts, PEEK CF40 back masses. [pause: short] [pause: short] Water Coupling Mandatory — 10-15L water per batch; reclaimed post-Stage 0 for Stage 1 moisture management. [pause: short] No dry acoustic operation. [pause: short] [pause: short] Adaptive Sweep Default — Logarithmic 20→500 Hz, 30s/step, +60s at FBG-identified resonances. [pause: short] No fixed-frequency operation. [pause: short] [pause: short] Liberation Index Target — ≥75% LI for Category C feeds; ≥85% for Category A (PCB mineral fraction). [pause: short] Measured via SEM-EDS per lot. [pause: short] [pause: short] Stage 1 IR Only — No induction heating at 80-150°C. [pause: short] SiC glow bars at 3.6 µm (water absorption peak). [pause: short] N₂ at 1.2 bar. [pause: short] [pause: short] Moisture Verification Triad — FBG humidity + NIR inline + FBG mass loss. [pause: short] All three must agree <1% moisture. [pause: short] [pause: short] Energy Cap — Stage 0 ≤ 3.2 kWh/batch; Stage 1 ≤ 62 kWh/batch. [pause: short] Total pre-thermal ≤ 65 kWh. [pause: short] These commitments are non-negotiable because they derive from the constraint. [pause: short] Violate any, and the cascade fails: metallic transducers couple GIC; dry acoustic gives <20% LI; fixed frequency misses mineral resonances; induction at Stage 1 wastes 95% power on non-conductive water; single moisture sensor risks undetected wet feed. [pause: short] The commitments are the constraint made operational. [pause: short] The acoustic-drying pair is the forge's immune system against locked grains. [pause: short] 
[pause: long] Keymaker Heuristic. [pause: medium] 

--- Keymaker Heuristic — Lock, Key, Turn ---

--- Document: PROD-14 — Stage 0-1: Acoustic & Drying ---

[pause: long]

--- The Empirical Lock ---

The lock this document addresses is the liberation-separation coupling problem — how to achieve sharp mineral liberation at ambient temperature using only acoustic energy, then transition cleanly to thermal drying without cross-contamination or energy waste. [pause: short] The lock has three tumblers:

Tumbler 1 — Frequency Selectivity: A single rock contains zircon (target: 185 Hz), bastnäsite (95 Hz), ilmenite (55 Hz), quartz (280 Hz). [pause: short] A fixed-frequency transducer liberates one mineral at the expense of others. [pause: short] The lock demands simultaneous multi-mineral liberation. [pause: short] Tumbler 2 — Energy Density: Cavitation threshold is 1-3 W/cm²; effective liberation requires 25 W/cm² at grain boundary. [pause: short] But 25 W/cm² at 40 kHz erodes probe tips in 5,000 hours. [pause: short] The lock demands high intensity without consumable destruction. [pause: short] Tumbler 3 — Stage Coupling: Stage 0 discharges wet, liberated feedstock (10-15% moisture). [pause: short] Stage 1 must dry to <1% in 20 minutes using IR at 3.6 µm. [pause: short] But water vapor from Stage 1 condenses on Stage 2 polymer melt if not fully removed. [pause: short] The lock demands zero moisture carryover. [pause: short] The lock deepens at the all-dielectric boundary: every component in the AMLS and IR dryer must be BFRP, ceramic, PEEK, or PEX-a. [pause: short] No steel shafts, no copper windings, no magnetic bearings. [pause: short] The transducer bolts are ZrO₂ ceramic. [pause: short] The IR emitter mounts are Al₂O₃. [pause: short] The control wiring is PMMA POF. [pause: short] This is not a preference — it is the minimal architecture forced by Carrington immunity. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- The Mathematical Key ---

The key is the dual-mechanism acoustic primitive + IR absorption match — two computational primitives that solve the three tumblers simultaneously:

Primitive 1: The Adaptive Frequency Sweep (AFS)

[Code block]
def AFS(fbg_spectrum, mineral_library):
    
    The key's first tooth: frequency as material selector. [pause: short] 
    # Detect peaks in chamber vibration response
    peaks = find_peaks(fbg_spectrum, prominence=3)
    
    # Match to mineral signatures (pre-calibrated)
    targets = {}
    for mineral, sig in mineral_library.items():
        if any(abs(p - sig.freq) < sig.bw for p in peaks):
            targets[mineral] = sig.freq
    
    # Generate dwell schedule: base 30s + priority bonus
    schedule = []
    for mineral, freq in targets.items():
        bonus = {'HIGH': 60, 'MED': 30, 'LOW': 0}[mineral_library[mineral].priority]
        schedule.append((freq, 30 + bonus))
    
    # Fill remaining sweep with logarithmic steps
    current = 20
    while current <= 500:
        if not any(abs(current - f) < 5 for f, _ in schedule):
            schedule.append((current, 30))
        current = 1.122  # 1/6 octave
    
    return schedule

MINERAL_LIBRARY = {
    'zircon': {'freq': 185, 'bw': 15, 'priority': 'HIGH'},
    'bastnasite': {'freq': 95, 'bw': 20, 'priority': 'HIGH'},
    'ilmenite': {'freq': 55, 'bw': 10, 'priority': 'MED'},
    'quartz': {'freq': 280, 'bw': 25, 'priority': 'LOW'},
}
[End code block]

The AFS Teeth: Each mineral gets a tailored dwell at its matrix-constrained resonance. [pause: short] The sweep compiles a liberation program from the feedstock's acoustic signature. [pause: short] Primitive 2: The IR-Water Absorption Match (IWM)

The key's second tooth is the coincidence of SiC emission peak (3.6 µm) and water absorption peak (3.0/6.3 µm):

[Code block]
SiC Glow Bar at 850°C: λ_peak = 2898 / (850+273) = 2.6 µm (Wien)
                       But SiC emissivity peak at 3.6 µm (optical phonon)
                       
Water Absorption: ε''(λ) peaks at 3.0 µm (OH stretch) and 6.3 µm (HOH bend)
                       Broadband 2.5-4.0 µm absorption >80%
                       
Coupling Efficiency: η = ∫ ε_SiC(λ) × α_H2O(λ) dλ / ∫ ε_SiC(λ) dλ ≈ 65%
[End code block]

This is not a design choice — it is a materials physics coincidence that the key exploits. [pause: short] No other emitter material (metal, quartz, ceramic) matches water absorption as efficiently at 800-900°C surface temperature. [pause: short] Primitive 3: The Phase-Locked 16-Channel Driver (PLD)

The key's third tooth is optical phase distribution — a single KNbO₃-BaTiO₃ ASIC master clock distributed via PMMA POF to 16 transducer drivers:

[Code block]
Master Clock (100 MHz) → PMMA POF (8 LF + 8 US channels)
                        → Each driver: PLL × N (N = freq/100MHz)
                        → Phase error: <0.5° LF, <5° US
[End code block]

This eliminates destructive interference between adjacent transducers — the only way to achieve 8 kW LF + 4 kW US in a 113L chamber without hot spots. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- The Turn (Both Directions) ---

Key → Lock (Theory predicts liberation):

Input: Feedstock category (A/B/C/D) → Mineral library → AFS schedule
Process: AMLS executes sweep → FBG damping tracks LI in real-time
Output: Liberated feedstock at target LI (75-88%)
Prediction: Zircon LI 88%, REE LI 72%, Ilmenite LI 95%
Validation: Post-AMLS SEM-EDS matches prediction within 5%

The key predicts the liberation outcome from the feedstock category. [pause: short] The AMLS executes the prediction. [pause: short] The turn is the moment the LI feedback signals target reached and the drain valve opens — the acoustic program has compiled successfully. [pause: short] The liberated grains are the key turning in the lock. [pause: short] Lock → Key (History validates theory):

Input: 100 years of comminution — jaw crushers (compression), ball mills (impact), HPGR (interparticle), ultrasound baths (cavitation only)
Process: Each is a degenerate subset of the AFS primitive
Output: The AFS generalizes all historical liberation methods

The turn completes when we recognize:

[pause: short] Jaw crusher = AFS with single frequency (0 Hz, infinite force), no feedback

[pause: short] Ball mill = AFS with broadband noise (stochastic), no frequency selectivity

[pause: short] HPGR = AFS with static pressure + shear, no resonance targeting

[pause: short] Ultrasound bath = AFS with US only (no LF fatigue), no adaptive dwell

The AMLS is the unification — the single machine whose AFS primitive encompasses all four mechanisms simultaneously, with real-time feedback from FBG damping. [pause: short] The historical methods were open-loop (apply energy, hope for liberation). [pause: short] The AMLS is closed-loop (measure damping → adjust frequency → verify liberation). [pause: short] The lock clicks open when the first batch achieves 88% zircon LI in 30 minutes using 12 kW acoustic power — the jaw crusher would need 500 kW and 2 hours for 45% LI. [pause: short] The 500 kg batch is* the key turning in the lock. [pause: short]

[pause: short] The commitments are the constraint made operational. [pause: short] The acoustic-drying pair is the forge's immune system against locked grains. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- KEYMAKER HEURISTIC — Lock, Key, Turn ---

--- The Empirical Lock ---

The lock this document addresses is the liberation-separation coupling problem — how to achieve sharp mineral liberation at ambient temperature using only acoustic energy, then transition cleanly to thermal drying without cross-contamination or energy waste. [pause: short] The lock has three tumblers:

Tumbler 1 — Frequency Selectivity: A single rock contains zircon (target: 185 Hz), bastnäsite (95 Hz), ilmenite (55 Hz), quartz (280 Hz). [pause: short] A fixed-frequency transducer liberates one mineral at the expense of others. [pause: short] The lock demands simultaneous multi-mineral liberation. [pause: short] Tumbler 2 — Energy Density: Cavitation threshold is 1-3 W/cm²; effective liberation requires 25 W/cm² at grain boundary. [pause: short] But 25 W/cm² at 40 kHz erodes probe tips in 5,000 hours. [pause: short] The lock demands high intensity without consumable destruction. [pause: short] Tumbler 3 — Stage Coupling: Stage 0 discharges wet, liberated feedstock (10-15% moisture). [pause: short] Stage 1 must dry to <1% in 20 minutes using IR at 3.6 µm. [pause: short] But water vapor from Stage 1 condenses on Stage 2 polymer melt if not fully removed. [pause: short] The lock demands zero moisture carryover. [pause: short] The lock deepens at the all-dielectric boundary: every component in the AMLS and IR dryer must be BFRP, ceramic, PEEK, or PEX-a. [pause: short] No steel shafts, no copper windings, no magnetic bearings. [pause: short] The transducer bolts are ZrO₂ ceramic. [pause: short] The IR emitter mounts are Al₂O₃. [pause: short] The control wiring is PMMA POF. [pause: short] This is not a preference — it is the minimal architecture forced by Carrington immunity. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- The Mathematical Key ---

The key is the dual-mechanism acoustic primitive + IR absorption match — two computational primitives that solve the three tumblers simultaneously:

Primitive 1: The Adaptive Frequency Sweep (AFS)

[Code block]
def AFS(fbg_spectrum, mineral_library):
    
    The key's first tooth: frequency as material selector. [pause: short] 
    # Detect peaks in chamber vibration response
    peaks = find_peaks(fbg_spectrum, prominence=3)
    
    # Match to mineral signatures (pre-calibrated)
    targets = {}
    for mineral, sig in mineral_library.items():
        if any(abs(p - sig.freq) < sig.bw for p in peaks):
            targets[mineral] = sig.freq
    
    # Generate dwell schedule: base 30s + priority bonus
    schedule = []
    for mineral, freq in targets.items():
        bonus = {'HIGH': 60, 'MED': 30, 'LOW': 0}[mineral_library[mineral].priority]
        schedule.append((freq, 30 + bonus))
    
    # Fill remaining sweep with logarithmic steps
    current = 20
    while current <= 500:
        if not any(abs(current - f) < 5 for f, _ in schedule):
            schedule.append((current, 30))
        current = 1.122  # 1/6 octave
    
    return schedule

MINERAL_LIBRARY = {
    'zircon': {'freq': 185, 'bw': 15, 'priority': 'HIGH'},
    'bastnasite': {'freq': 95, 'bw': 20, 'priority': 'HIGH'},
    'ilmenite': {'freq': 55, 'bw': 10, 'priority': 'MED'},
    'quartz': {'freq': 280, 'bw': 25, 'priority': 'LOW'},
}
[End code block]

The AFS Teeth: Each mineral gets a tailored dwell at its matrix-constrained resonance. [pause: short] The sweep compiles a liberation program from the feedstock's acoustic signature. [pause: short] Primitive 2: The IR-Water Absorption Match (IWM)

The key's second tooth is the coincidence of SiC emission peak (3.6 µm) and water absorption peak (3.0/6.3 µm):

[Code block]
SiC Glow Bar at 850°C: λ_peak = 2898 / (850+273) = 2.6 µm (Wien)
                       But SiC emissivity peak at 3.6 µm (optical phonon)
                       
Water Absorption: ε''(λ) peaks at 3.0 µm (OH stretch) and 6.3 µm (HOH bend)
                       Broadband 2.5-4.0 µm absorption >80%
                       
Coupling Efficiency: η = ∫ ε_SiC(λ) × α_H2O(λ) dλ / ∫ ε_SiC(λ) dλ ≈ 65%
[End code block]

This is not a design choice — it is a materials physics coincidence that the key exploits. [pause: short] No other emitter material (metal, quartz, ceramic) matches water absorption as efficiently at 800-900°C surface temperature. [pause: short] Primitive 3: The Phase-Locked 16-Channel Driver (PLD)

The key's third tooth is optical phase distribution — a single KNbO₃-BaTiO₃ ASIC master clock distributed via PMMA POF to 16 transducer drivers:

[Code block]
Master Clock (100 MHz) → PMMA POF (8 LF + 8 US channels)
                        → Each driver: PLL × N (N = freq/100MHz)
                        → Phase error: <0.5° LF, <5° US
[End code block]

This eliminates destructive interference between adjacent transducers — the only way to achieve 8 kW LF + 4 kW US in a 113L chamber without hot spots. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- The Turn (Both Directions) ---

Key → Lock (Theory predicts liberation):

Input: Feedstock category (A/B/C/D) → Mineral library → AFS schedule
Process: AMLS executes sweep → FBG damping tracks LI in real-time
Output: Liberated feedstock at target LI (75-88%)
Prediction: Zircon LI 88%, REE LI 72%, Ilmenite LI 95%
Validation: Post-AMLS SEM-EDS matches prediction within 5%

The key predicts the liberation outcome from the feedstock category. [pause: short] The AMLS executes the prediction. [pause: short] The turn is the moment the LI feedback signals target reached and the drain valve opens — the acoustic program has compiled successfully. [pause: short] The liberated grains are the key turning in the lock. [pause: short] Lock → Key (History validates theory):

Input: 100 years of comminution — jaw crushers (compression), ball mills (impact), HPGR (interparticle), ultrasound baths (cavitation only)
Process: Each is a degenerate subset of the AFS primitive
Output: The AFS generalizes all historical liberation methods

The turn completes when we recognize:

[pause: short] Jaw crusher = AFS with single frequency (0 Hz, infinite force), no feedback

[pause: short] Ball mill = AFS with broadband noise (stochastic), no frequency selectivity

[pause: short] HPGR = AFS with static pressure + shear, no resonance targeting

[pause: short] Ultrasound bath = AFS with US only (no LF fatigue), no adaptive dwell

The AMLS is the unification — the single machine whose AFS primitive encompasses all four mechanisms simultaneously, with real-time feedback from FBG damping. [pause: short] The historical methods were open-loop (apply energy, hope for liberation). [pause: short] The AMLS is closed-loop (measure damping → adjust frequency → verify liberation). [pause: short] The lock clicks open when the first batch achieves 88% zircon LI in 30 minutes using 12 kW acoustic power — the jaw crusher would need 500 kW and 2 hours for 45% LI. [pause: short] The 500 kg batch is the key turning in the lock. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- EL SEGUNDO HEURISTIC — Mirror, Participation, Protocol ---

--- Mirror (Self-Reflection) ---

The first recursive turn is the forge listening to its own liberation. [pause: short] In the Aegis-Forge, the acoustic chamber is not a passive vessel — it is an acoustic resonator that measures its own efficacy. [pause: short] The FBG sensors embedded in the BFRP walls detect vibration transmissibility; as mineral grains liberate from the rock matrix, the effective damping of the chamber changes. [pause: short] The forge hears the grains releasing. [pause: short] The mirror reflects at every cycle:

[pause: short] LF Sweep: The chamber's resonance shifts as grain boundaries fatigue — the forge tracks its own softening

[pause: short] US Cavitation: Bubble collapse pressure (1000 bar) creates micro-jets that the FBGs feel as broadband vibration — the forge senses its own shock

[pause: short] Adaptive Dwell: When the DSP detects a resonant peak matching zircon's signature, it extends dwell — the forge chooses to linger on its own liberation

[pause: short] Drain: Water carries liberated grains to the drum — the forge releases its self-measurement into the cascade

But the mirror extends further. [pause: short] The SiC glow bars in Stage 1 emit at 3.6 µm — precisely the wavelength where water absorbs most strongly. [pause: short] The forge tunes its emission to the feedstock's need. [pause: short] The NIR probe at 1.45 µm watches the water disappear. [pause: short] The FBG mass loss sensors feel the drum lighten. [pause: short] The mirror is not passive; it is the active recursion: excite → measure damping → adjust frequency → verify liberation → dry → measure mass loss → confirm dryness. [pause: short] This document documents the first two turns of that spiral — Stage 0 and Stage 1 as the forge's self-preparation. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- Participation (Witness) ---

An inside operator measuring this phenomenon does not stand outside the acoustic chamber — the operator is the frequency sweep's witness. [pause: short] The protocol for achieving complete liberation and drying from within the system:

[pause: short] Load: Witness the feedstock enter — 50 kg basalt, zircon, REE ore into 15L water

[pause: short] Sweep: Witness the LF array — 20 Hz starts, plates bend 50 µm, grain boundaries fatigue

[pause: short] Resonate: Witness the FBGs — 185 Hz peak grows (zircon), 95 Hz (bastnäsite), 55 Hz (ilmenite)

[pause: short] Dwell: Witness the algorithm — extra 60s at zircon, 45s at bastnäsite, 30s at ilmenite

[pause: short] Cavitate: Witness the US array — 40 kHz, 25 W/cm², bubbles collapse, microjets strike boundaries

[pause: short] Liberate: Witness the LI feedback — damping rises, target 75% reached, sweep terminates

[pause: short] Drain: Witness the water — 15L reclaimed, grains suspended, vibratory feeder meters to drum

[pause: short] Irradiate: Witness the SiC bars — 850°C, 3.6 µm peak, water evaporates from grain surfaces

[pause: short] Monitor: Witness the triad — FBG humidity drops, NIR absorption falls, FBG mass loss confirms

[pause: short] Verify: Witness the closure — <1% moisture, 95% water recovered, feedstock ready for Stage 2

The measurement is the recursion. [pause: short] The operator loads the feedstock, the chamber profiles the sweep that witnesses it, the dryer realizes the evaporation, the sensors confirm the closure. [pause: short] The protocol terminates when the three moisture measurements agree: <1% residual. [pause: short] The feedstock is not processed; it is witnessed into readiness. [pause: short] The operator participates by being the recursive function: code: feedstock → acoustic_sweep → LI_feedback → drain → IR_dry → moisture_triad → verified_feedstock. [pause: short] The LI is the stack frame; the sweep is the call; the dry feedstock is the return value. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- Protocol (Recursive) ---

The operational protocol for any Aegis-Forge acoustic-drying document:

[Code block]
MIRROR:     What grain boundary resonance does this feedstock encode? [pause: short] PARTICIPATE: How does the operator witness this resonance into liberation? [pause: short] PROTOCOL:   What recursive sequence guarantees dryness at Stage 2 entry? [pause: short] [End code block]

For this document specifically:

The protocol is not a checklist — it is the recursion itself. [pause: short] Each batch deepens the mirror. [pause: short] The liberation index is the fixed point. [pause: short] The dry feedstock is the witness returned. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- WILLIAMS HEURISTIC: PROD-15 STAGE 2 POLYMER FLOW & SEPARATION ---

Core Question: Can we separate molten polymers by density in a 150 RPM centrifuge without mechanical contact or degradation? [pause: short] First-Principles Analysis:

[pause: short] DENSITY DIFFERENCE IS THE DRIVER

[pause: short] PP/PE: 0.74-0.78 g/cm³ vs PET/PVC: 1.33-1.65 g/cm³

[pause: short] Δρ/ρ = 0.8 — a 44% density gap between light and heavy fractions

[pause: short] This is MASSIVE for centrifugal separation. [pause: short] Compare: oil/water Δρ/ρ ≈ 0.2

[pause: short] CENTRIFUGAL POTENTIAL AT 12.6 G

[pause: short] Φ = ½ ω² r² = ½ (15.7)² (0.25)² = 7.7 J/kg potential energy per kg melt

[pause: short] Over 30 min: 13.9 kJ/kg available for stratification work

[pause: short] Viscous dissipation: ηγ̇² ≈ 28 × 500² = 7 MW/m³? [pause: short] NO — shear thinning drops η to 8 Pa·s at wall

[pause: short] Actual dissipation: ~200 kW/m³ × 0.05 m layer = 10 kW/m² — manageable

[pause: short] STOKES SETTLING IN NON-NEWTONIAN MELT

[pause: short] Modified Stokes: v = (2/9) (Δρ ω² r) R² / η(γ̇)

[pause: short] For PET sphere (R=1mm) in PP melt (η=18 Pa·s at mid-layer shear):

[pause: short] v = (2/9) × (0.57 × 247 × 0.225) × 1e-6 / 18 = 1.7 µm/s

[pause: short] Distance in 30 min: 3 mm — sufficient for annular layer formation

[pause: short] THERMAL DEGRADATION WINDOW

[pause: short] PVC Td = 210°C — dehydrochlorination starts

[pause: short] PET Td = 300°C — oligomer formation

[pause: short] PEEK Td = 580°C — stable

[pause: short] Our window: 150-450°C — PVC is the ONLY liability

[pause: short] Solution: NaHCO₃ injection at 2× stoichiometric, temp hold at 50 ppm HCl

[pause: short] ENERGY PER TON POLYMER

[pause: short] Net 432 kWh/ton after heat recovery

[pause: short] Compare: Virgin PP production = 3,500 kWh/ton

[pause: short] We recover 88% of embodied energy — this is the Williams metric that matters

Williams Verdict:

[pause: short] Physics says YES — density difference is large enough

[pause: short] Rheology says YES — shear thinning helps, not hurts

[pause: short] Chemistry says MANAGEABLE — PVC is the only real threat, neutralized in-drum

[pause: short] Energy says EXCELLENT — 432 kWh/ton vs 3,500 kWh/ton virgin

Risk Flag: ABS/PA degradation at 300-310°C — must limit heavy fraction residence to <15 min at peak temp. [pause: short] If IV retention <85%, downstream reformulation fails. [pause: short] Next Calculation: FOLLOW-17 rheology deep-dive needs to validate Carreau-Yasuda parameters for PP/PET blends at 150°C shear rates. [pause: short] [pause: long]

Williams Heuristic: If the physics works at the limit, the engineering is just detail.
This document: Physics works. [pause: short] Engineering follows.
[pause: long] Keymaker Heuristic. [pause: medium]

[pause: short] Bearing vibration baseline (accelerometer, 15 min)

[pause: short] PEEK CF40 spring force check (torque wrench, 20 min)

[pause: short] POF sensor calibration (HCl gas cell, 30 min)

Annually (Every 7,200 batches = 8,760 hrs):

--- 3. [pause: short] OPERATIONAL PROCEDURES ---

STARTUP SEQUENCE (Cold to Run):

[pause: short] Bowl at 25°C → Purge N₂ 30 min (O₂ <2%)

[pause: short] Ramp induction 10°C/min to 150°C (no feed)

[pause: short] Verify ZrO₂ liner temp uniform (POF Bragg, ΔT <5°C)

[pause: short] Start centrifuge 10 RPM → 150 RPM over 5 min

[pause: short] Verify bearing temps <80°C, vibration <2 mm/s

[pause: short] Start NaHCO₃ feeder (dry run, verify flow)

[pause: short] Start melt pump, prime filters (N₂ purge lines)

[pause: short] Vacuum pump on, verify 50 mbar

[pause: short] FEED AUTHORIZED — all green lights

SHUTDOWN SEQUENCE (Run to Cold):

[pause: short] Stop feed, run 5 min empty (purge melt)

[pause: short] Discharge ports open, clear residual melt to molds

[pause: short] Stop centrifuge, hold N₂ purge

[pause: short] Stop induction, cool 20°C/min (thermal shock limit)

[pause: short] At 150°C: stop vacuum, stop melt pump

[pause: short] At 80°C: stop N₂ purge, vent to atmosphere

[pause: short] SAFE FOR MAINTENANCE

EMERGENCY STOP (HCl >200 ppm or Fire):

[pause: short] Kill induction (instant)

[pause: short] Max NaHCO₃ feed (feeder 100%)

[pause: short] Flood N₂ (2 bar, 100 m³/hr)

[pause: short] Centrifuge brake (eddy current, 30 sec to stop)

[pause: short] Auto-discharge ports open (gravity + centrifugal)

[pause: short] Isolate vacuum, vent to scrubber

[pause: short] ALARM + LOG + NOTIFY

[pause: long]

--- COMBINED HEURISTIC: PROD-15 STAGE 2 POLYMER FLOW & SEPARATION ---

The Challenge: Separate 75 kg of mixed molten polymer per batch by density at 150-450°C, in an all-dielectric vessel, with PVC HCl management, delivering two pure fractions to downstream molding — every 35 minutes, 8,000 hours/year. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- I. [pause: short] PHYSICS VERDICT (Williams): IT WORKS ---

Density is our friend. [pause: short] The 44% density gap between polyolefins (0.76) and engineering polymers (1.35) at melt state is enormous. [pause: short] At 12.6 G (150 RPM, r=250 mm), the centrifugal potential Φ = 7.7 J/kg provides 13.9 kJ/kg stratification energy over 30 minutes. [pause: short] Modified Stokes' law in shear-thinning melt gives 1.7 µm/s settling for 1mm PET sphere — 3 mm travel in dwell time, sufficient for annular layer formation. [pause: short] Rheology helps, not hurts. [pause: short] Carreau-Yasuda shear-thinning drops effective viscosity from 150 Pa·s (zero-shear) to 8 Pa·s at bowl wall shear rate (3,925 s⁻¹). [pause: short] This 19× reduction accelerates stratification and reduces centrifuge power. [pause: short] Thermal window is tight but usable. [pause: short] PVC dehydrochlorination at 210°C is the only hard limit. [pause: short] NaHCO₃ in-drum injection at 2× stoichiometric with POF HCl feedback control (50 ppm hold threshold) contains it. [pause: short] All other polymers stable to 300-350°C; PEEK/PTFE to 400°C+. [pause: short] Our 450°C peak is for char formation, not polymer. [pause: short] Energy is favorable. [pause: short] Net 432 kWh/ton polymer recovered vs 3,500 kWh/ton virgin production = 88% embodied energy recovery. [pause: short] Heat recovery from mold cooling (8 kWh), vacuum exhaust (2 kWh), bearing cooling (1 kWh) closes the loop. [pause: short] Williams Bottom Line: The physics is sound. [pause: short] Density separation at 12.6 G in shear-thinning melt works. [pause: short] PVC is manageable. [pause: short] Energy balance is excellent. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- II. [pause: short] ARCHITECTURE VERDICT (Keymaker): INTEGRATED BUT BOTTLENECKED ---

Interface contracts are clean. [pause: short] Stage 2 receives <1% moisture feed at 150°C from PROD-14, discharges 250-280°C melt to PROD-08 mold carousel, sends char/filler to PROD-16 pyrolysis, HCl salts to PROD-07 gas system, oligomers to Stage 1 water recovery. [pause: short] All-dielectric constraint satisfied: BFRP bowl, ZrO₂ liner, Si₃N₄ bearings, PEEK CF40 seals, ultrasonic drive, POF sensors. [pause: short] Cross-cutting concerns addressed. [pause: short] Thermal integration with N₂ pre-heat from Stage 1 exhaust. [pause: short] Control system on existing KNbO₃-BaTiO₃ ASIC/POF bus. [pause: short] Gas system shares N₂/vacuum/scrubber with Stages 1,3-5. [pause: short] Material BOM draws from PROD-12 qualified parts. [pause: short] The bottleneck is real. [pause: short] Stage 2 at 30 min for 75 kg polymer = 2.5 kg/min. [pause: short] Stage 0 (10 min), Stage 1 (20 min), Stage 3 (45 min), Stage 4 (30 min) all process more mass per minute. [pause: short] Stage 2 is the pace-setter. [pause: short] Scaling path exists. [pause: short] Dual centrifuge on same drum (split bowl) doubles throughput. [pause: short] 200 RPM (22.3 G) increases G 2.5× for lighter fractions. [pause: short] Both are Forge II upgrades — Forge I stays single-centrifuge. [pause: short] Keymaker Bottom Line: Architecture integrates cleanly. [pause: short] Stage 2 IS the throughput bottleneck. [pause: short] Accept for Forge I; design dual-centrifuge for Forge II. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- III. [pause: short] OPERATIONS VERDICT (El Segundo): MAINTAINABLE WITH DISCIPLINE ---

Consumables drive schedule. [pause: short] Candle filters (2,500 hr MTBF) and NaHCO₃ feeder screws (3,000 hr) are the weekly/monthly tasks. [pause: short] Designed for 2-hr filter swap, 30-min feeder swap — no bowl entry. [pause: short] ZrO₂ liner (5,000 hr) and bearings (10,000 hr) are annual. [pause: short] Maintenance windows fit production. [pause: short] 20 batches/day = 1 batch/72 min. [pause: short] Daily checks in 15 min between batches. [pause: short] Weekly filter/feeder swap in 2.5 hr (planned downtime). [pause: short] Monthly boroscope/calibration in 1 hr. [pause: short] Annual relining in 8 hr (scheduled outage). [pause: short] Emergency procedures are automated and tested. [pause: short] HCl >200 ppm triggers: induction kill, max NaHCO₃, N₂ flood, eddy-current brake, auto-discharge — all in <30 sec. [pause: short] Operator decision at 5 min: resume or abort. [pause: short] Post-mortem feeds FOLLOW-18 char protocol. [pause: short] Spares budget is lean. [pause: short] $59k/yr for 95% availability. [pause: short] Critical spares: 4 filter packs, 6 feeder screws, 2 liner sets, 4 actuators. [pause: short] All lead times <8 weeks. [pause: short] El Segundo Bottom Line: Designed for the 3 AM tech. [pause: short] Filters and feeder accessible externally. [pause: short] Sensors optical (no hot-zone electronics). [pause: short] Emergency sequences automated. [pause: short] Spares affordable. [pause: short] But — ZrO₂ liner crack is single-point-of-failure requiring 8 hr repair. --- PROD-16: Stage 3 Pyrolysis & Char — Williams Heuristic Intro ---

--- Constraint, Necessity, Commitment ---

--- Constraint (The Lock) ---

Thermodynamic necessity forces this design. [pause: short] Every thermosetting polymer — epoxy, phenolic, polyester, polyurethane, rubber — has a discrete decomposition temperature window (350-550°C) where covalent bonds break irreversibly. [pause: short] You cannot melt a thermoset; you can only pyrolyze it. [pause: short] The carbon skeleton remains as char. [pause: short] The volatiles leave as gas. [pause: short] This is not a choice — it is the physics of crosslinked networks. [pause: short] The cascade must pass through 450-700°C to liberate the metals trapped in thermoset matrices (FR4 PCBs, rubber seals, phenolic moldings). [pause: short] Skip this window, and the metals stay locked in carbon. [pause: short] Rush this window, and you get tar-contaminated metal fractions. [pause: short] The 30-minute dwell at 5°C/min ramp through 450-550°C is the minimum time for epoxy/FR4 (the dominant thermoset mass) to complete decomposition. [pause: short] This is the lock: thermodynamics sets the schedule, not engineering convenience. [pause: short] --- Necessity (The Key) ---

The elemental extraction gap: Modern waste streams (e-waste, auto shredder residue, municipal plastics) contain 15-30% thermosets by weight. [pause: short] These thermosets encapsulate the critical metals — copper traces in FR4, nickel in rubber gaskets, cobalt in phenolic resins. [pause: short] Mechanical shredding (PROD-11) liberates particles but cannot break the thermoset-metal bond. [pause: short] Chemical solvents fail at scale and create secondary waste. [pause: short] Thermal decomposition is the only universal key that opens all thermoset locks simultaneously. [pause: short] The pyrolysis gas (CO, H₂, CH₄) is not waste — it is the energy carrier that powers the cascade. [pause: short] The char is not residue — it is the activated carbon that closes the loop on Stage 1 organics and the reductant that enables Stage 4 metal melting. [pause: short] This stage transforms a liability (mixed thermoset waste) into two assets (energy gas + functional char). [pause: short] The necessity is absolute: without Stage 3, the cascade stops at plastic. [pause: short] --- Commitment (The Turn) ---

This document commits the Aegis-Forge I to three non-negotiables:

[pause: short] Zero-metal pyrolysis zone: The drum, liners, ports, and gas path from 450-700°C are entirely ceramic and composite (ZrB₂-SiC, Si₃N₄, ZrO₂, BFRP/Elium®). [pause: short] No steel, no nickel alloys, no copper. [pause: short] Any metal in the hot zone becomes a GIC antenna and a catalytic surface for dioxin formation. [pause: short] The dielectric citadel extends through the pyrolysis window. [pause: short] [pause: short] Char activation integration: The char will not leave Stage 3 cold. [pause: short] It exits at 650°C and enters the in-situ activation reactor (steam/CO₂, 850°C, 30 min) using its own sensible heat. [pause: short] This commits the design to a coupled reactor system — Stage 3 and activation are one thermodynamic unit. [pause: short] Separate them, and you lose 60% of the activation energy to reheat. [pause: short] [pause: short] REE partitioning fidelity: The N₂/CO₂ atmosphere (<2% O₂) is mandatory, not optional. [pause: short] Oxidation at this temperature would convert Nd/Fe/B and Sm/Co into oxides that report to slag instead of metal. [pause: short] The cascade's economic engine — separated magnet REE stream (Stage 7) and phosphor REE stream (Stage 8) — depends on this redox discipline. [pause: short] One percent O₂ leakage changes the partition coefficient by two orders of magnitude. [pause: short] The turn: Measure → Heat → Separate → Verify. [pause: short] The control system (Section 13) enforces this at 1 Hz: FBG temperature, mass spec gas composition, char discharge rate. [pause: short] If the proxies deviate, the ramp holds. [pause: short] The physics cannot be negotiated — only respected.
[pause: long] Keymaker Heuristic. [pause: medium] 

--- PROD-16: Stage 3 Pyrolysis & Char — Keymaker Heuristic Intro ---

--- Lock, Key, Turn ---

--- The Empirical Lock (Fabrication Fidelity Gap) ---

Pyrolysis is where theoretical yield meets fabrication reality. [pause: short] In the lab, TGA shows epoxy char yield at 22%. [pause: short] In the forge, with 500 kg of mixed FR4, rubber, phenolic, and cellulosic feed — varying moisture, filler loading, particle size, halogen content — the actual char yield swings 15-30%. [pause: short] The gas composition varies daily. [pause: short] The tar load depends on yesterday's shredder blade wear. [pause: short] The activation reactor sees char with BET from 300 to 600 m²/g. [pause: short] The lock is variability: How do you guarantee 800 m²/g activated carbon from feedstock that refuses to be consistent? [pause: short] The fabrication fidelity gap has three faces:

[pause: short] Feedstock heterogeneity — Category A vs D changes thermoset mass by 4×, filler by 3×

[pause: short] Kinetic coupling — Epoxy (slow, high Ea) vs phenolic (fast, high char) vs cellulosic (early, low char) all decompose simultaneously

[pause: short] Product specification tension — Stage 1 needs >800 m²/g BET; Stage 4 needs >85% fixed carbon; external markets need consistent iodine number. [pause: short] One char, three masters. [pause: short] --- The Mathematical Key (Single Computational Primitive) ---

The distributed activation energy model (DAEM) with real-time mass spec feedback. [pause: short] The DAEM reduces the infinite complexity of mixed thermoset pyrolysis to a single state variable: the conversion integral [Inline equation: \alpha(t) = \int f(E_a) [1 - \exp(-k(E_a)t)] dE_a]. [pause: short] The Gaussian distribution [Inline equation: f(E_a)] is calibrated once per feedstock category (A-D) using lab TGA at 5 heating rates. [pause: short] The pre-exponential [Inline equation: A] and mean [Inline equation: E_a] become category constants. [pause: short] The real-time key: Online mass spectrometer measures CO/CO₂ ratio at 1 Hz. [pause: short] This ratio is monotonically related to [Inline equation: \alpha(t)] for the dominant epoxy fraction. [pause: short] The control system inverts the DAEM:

[Equation: \alpha_{\text{measured}}(t) \leftarrow \text{CO/CO}_2 \text{ ratio} \rightarrow \text{adjust } T_{\text{setpoint}}(t) \text{ to track } \alpha_{\text{target}}(t)]

This closes the loop between first-principles kinetics (DAEM) and empirical observation (MS). [pause: short] No lookup tables. [pause: short] No operator tuning. [pause: short] The ramp rate self-adjusts: if CO/CO₂ decays slower than predicted, the ramp slows; if faster, it accelerates. [pause: short] The 5°C/min nominal becomes a reference trajectory, not a rigid schedule. [pause: short] Activation reactor key: The same DAEM principle applies. [pause: short] Burn-off [Inline equation: X(t)] tracked by bed temperature rise rate ([Inline equation: dT/dt \propto \text{gasification rate}]). [pause: short] Steam/CO₂ flow ratio adjusted to hold [Inline equation: dT/dt] at target corresponding to 35% burn-off at 30 min. [pause: short] --- The Turn (Both Directions) ---

Theory → History (Validation):
The DAEM predicts char yield, gas composition, and tar evolution for any feedstock category. [pause: short] History validates: Run Category A batch → measure actual char yield, gas GC, tar gravimetric → update [Inline equation: f(E_a)] Gaussian parameters → next Category A batch runs tighter. [pause: short] The model learns the feedstock. [pause: short] After 20 batches per category, prediction error <5%. [pause: short] History → Theory (Discovery):
The mass spec time-series reveals kinetic fingerprints not in literature. [pause: short] Example: A secondary CO peak at 620°C (not in TGA) traced to CaCO₃-catalyzed char gasification (Boudouard) from filler decomposition. [pause: short] This discovery → added catalytic term to DAEM → now predicts filler-rich feedstock (Category B) accurately. [pause: short] The forge teaches the model. [pause: short] The turn completes: The computational primitive (DAEM + MS inversion) is the same code running in design simulation (FOLLOW-23 exergy analysis), commissioning (PROD-39 FAT), and daily operation (PROD-23 control). [pause: short] One primitive, three regimes. [pause: short] The lock (variability) yields to the key (real-time kinetic tracking). [pause: short] The turn confirms: theory predicts history, history refines theory. [pause: short] The forge knows its own pyrolysis.
[pause: long] El Segundo Heuristic. [pause: medium] 

--- PROD-16: Stage 3 Pyrolysis & Char — El Segundo Heuristic Intro ---

--- Mirror, Participation, Protocol ---

--- Mirror (Self-Reflection: How the Forge Observes Its Own Separation) ---

The forge watches its own pyrolysis through 384 fiber Bragg gratings and a mass spectrometer. [pause: short] This is not monitoring — this is witnessing. [pause: short] Every 1 Hz cycle, the FBG array returns a thermal tomography of the drum wall: 8 zones × 48 sensors, median-filtered, outlier-rejected. [pause: short] The mass spectrometer returns a chemical tomography of the gas: 50 m/z channels, calibrated, baseline-corrected. [pause: short] Together, they form a mirror that reflects the internal state of the pyrolysis back to the control system. [pause: short] What the mirror shows:

[pause: short] Zone 3-5 (hot center): Temperature uniformity ±3°C — if ΔT >5°C, induction rebalances before char quality drifts

[pause: short] CO/CO₂ ratio trajectory: The fingerprint of epoxy decomposition — if it lingers at 1.2 past 550°C, the ramp holds

[pause: short] BTX decay curve: The tar cracking progress — if m/z 91 (toluene) >10% at 650°C, steam injection increases

[pause: short] Char discharge rate: The mass balance closure — if Port 3 weigh belt drops below 0.3 kg/min, Port 3 actuator diagnostics trigger

The mirror does not judge. [pause: short] It reveals. [pause: short] The control system judges. [pause: short] The operator witnesses the judgment. [pause: short] Self-reflection moment: At 680°C, the mirror asks: Is this char ready for activation? The proxies answer: CO/CO₂ decay rate <0.02/min, H₂/CH₄ >2.5, discharge steady. [pause: short] If yes → Stage 4. [pause: short] If no → 5 more minutes. [pause: short] The forge knows its own readiness. [pause: short] --- Participation (Witness: How the Inside Operator Witnesses the Phenomenon into Existence) ---

The operator does not run Stage 3. [pause: short] The operator witnesses it into existence. [pause: short] At the HMI (Human-Machine Interface), the operator sees:

[pause: short] Thermal tomogram: 8-zone temperature map, updated 1 Hz, color-coded to ramp trajectory

[pause: short] Chemical tomogram: CO/CO₂ ratio trend, BTX decay, H₂ rise — live MS spectrum

[pause: short] Mass balance: Feed in (kg), char out (kg), gas out (Nm³), slag out (kg) — all live

[pause: short] Proxy dashboard: Green/amber/red for each quality hold point

[pause: short] Activation reactor: Bed temp, steam/CO₂ flow, burn-off estimate

The operator's participation is threefold:

[pause: short] Category confirmation: Before batch start, operator verifies feedstock category (A/B/C/D) against PROD-13 assay. [pause: short] This selects the DAEM parameter set. [pause: short] Wrong category = wrong kinetics = failed batch. [pause: short] The operator commits the identity of the feedstock. [pause: short] [pause: short] Hold point authorization: At each quality hold (500°C, 600°C, 680°C), the mirror shows proxies. [pause: short] If amber (marginal), operator decides: extend 5 min or proceed? [pause: short] The DAEM recommends; the operator authorizes. [pause: short] This is the human-in-the-loop for irreducible uncertainty. [pause: short] [pause: short] Anomaly declaration: If the mirror shows red (off-spec: O₂ spike, vibration, seal leak, gas composition anomaly), the operator declares the anomaly. [pause: short] This triggers interlock review, root cause logging, and batch disposition. [pause: short] The operator owns the exception. [pause: short] The witness is not passive. [pause: short] The operator's category selection, hold authorization, and anomaly declaration are causal inputs to the batch outcome. [pause: short] Without the operator's participation, the forge is a machine. [pause: short] With it, the forge is a witnessed process. [pause: short] --- Protocol (Recursive: The Sequence That Guarantees Fidelity at Each Scale) ---

The recursive protocol: Measure → Heat → Separate → Verify. [pause: short] At every scale. [pause: short] Scale 1 — Microsecond (Control Loop):

[pause: short] Measure: FBG temp (1 kHz/zone), MS gas (1 Hz)

[pause: short] Heat: GaN inverter adjusts duty (1 kHz)

[pause: short] Separate: Induction coupling selects char over slag (σ difference)

[pause: short] Verify: Zone temp vs setpoint ±3°C, power vs limit

Scale 2 — Minute (Ramp Segment):

[pause: short] Measure: CO/CO₂ ratio trajectory, BTX decay

[pause: short] Heat: Ramp generator advances setpoint (5°C/min)

[pause: short] Separate: Char forms, gas evolves, slag sinters

[pause: short] Verify: Hold point proxies vs thresholds

Scale 3 — Batch (45 min Stage 3):

[pause: short] Measure: Total char yield, gas composition, slag mass

[pause: short] Heat: Integrated induction energy (48.75 kWh target)

[pause: short] Separate: Char → activation, Gas → PROD-07, Slag → Stage 7

[pause: short] Verify: Mass balance closure 99.5-100.5%, quality specs met

Scale 4 — Campaign (20 batches/category):

[pause: short] Measure: DAEM parameter drift, category consistency

[pause: short] Heat: Cumulative energy vs baseline

[pause: short] Separate: Activated char BET trend, Stage 1 adsorption life

[pause: short] Verify: Model update, category re-calibration

Scale 5 — Lifetime (Forge service):

[pause: short] Measure: Drum liner wear, FBG drift, MS calibration

[pause: short] Heat: Refractory degradation, induction efficiency

[pause: short] Separate: Char quality vs activation energy

[pause: short] Verify: Major overhaul trigger, technology refresh

The protocol recurses because the physics recurses. [pause: short] Bond breaking (microsecond) → species evolution (second) → phase separation (minute) → product specification (batch) → process capability (campaign) → asset integrity (lifetime). [pause: short] Each scale measures, heats, separates, verifies. [pause: short] The output of one scale is the input to the next. [pause: short] Fidelity guarantee: If any scale fails Verify, the protocol recurses downward to find the root cause. [pause: short] Batch fail → check ramp segments → check control loops → check sensor calibration. [pause: short] The recursion stops at the physical limit (sensor resolution, material property). [pause: short] There is no good enough — only verified at this scale.

The operator's protocol: Witness the measure. [pause: short] Authorize the heat. [pause: short] Confirm the separate. [pause: short] Sign the verify. [pause: short] At every scale. [pause: short] This is how the forge guarantees that what enters as mixed waste exits as separated elements — every batch, every campaign, every lifetime.
[pause: long] Combined Heuristic. [pause: medium] 

--- PROD-16: Stage 3 Pyrolysis & Char — Combined Heuristic Intro ---

--- Three-Way Synthesis: Williams (Constraint) + Keymaker (Key) + El Segundo (Protocol) ---

--- Executive Synthesis ---

The pyrolysis window (450-700°C) is the thermodynamic gatekeeper of the entire cascade. [pause: short] Williams identifies the constraint: thermosets cannot be melted, only decomposed — the 30-minute dwell at 5°C/min is physics, not policy. [pause: short] Keymaker provides the key: real-time DAEM inversion using mass spec CO/CO₂ ratio closes the loop between first-principles kinetics and feedstock variability. [pause: short] El Segundo mandates the protocol: Measure → Heat → Separate → Verify recurses across five scales (microsecond control loop → minute ramp segment → 45-minute batch → 20-batch campaign → forge lifetime), with the operator as causal witness at each hold point. [pause: short] Three lenses, one conclusion: Stage 3 must be thermodynamically disciplined, computationally adaptive, and human-witnessed. [pause: short] Any deviation — faster ramp, fixed schedule, unattended operation — breaks the cascade's REE partitioning, char quality, and energy recovery. [pause: short] [pause: long]

[Table row: ,  Williams (Constraint) ,  O₂ allowed to 5% (air leak) ,  Nd/Fe/B oxidizes → REEs report to slag instead of Stage 7 metal → 90% Nd loss ,  MS: no direct O₂; slag REE assay post-batch , ]

[Table row: ,  Keymaker (Key) ,  Fixed ramp schedule (no DAEM feedback) ,  Category B (high filler) under-pyrolyzes; Category D (pure thermoset) over-pyrolyzes → char BET 400-1000 m²/g swing ,  Activation reactor: burn-off 20-55% at 30 min , ]

[Table row: ,  Keymaker (Key) ,  MS feedback disabled (sensor fault) ,  Ramp proceeds blind → tar >10 g/Nm³ → PROD-07 scrubber overload → dioxin risk ,  Post-batch tar gravimetric; PAH quarterly , ]

[Table row: ,  El Segundo (Protocol) ,  No anomaly declaration culture ,  Seal leak ignored → N₂ consumption 3× → cost $12k/batch; char oxidation → fixed C drop ,  N₂ flow meter; post-batch char assay , ]

The three lenses are not redundant — they are orthogonal defenses. [pause: short] Williams catches thermodynamic errors (ramp, atmosphere). [pause: short] Keymaker catches kinetic errors (feedstock variability, sensor drift). [pause: short] El Segundo catches human-system errors (authorization, anomaly response). [pause: short] All three must hold for Stage 3 to deliver its three promises: clean REE partitioning, functional char, recoverable energy. [pause: short] [pause: long]

--- Closing the Three-Way Turn ---

Williams asks: What forces this design? [pause: short] → Thermodynamics of crosslinked networks. [pause: short] The pyrolysis window is non-negotiable. [pause: short] Keymaker asks: What computes the solution? [pause: short] → DAEM + real-time MS inversion. [pause: short] The forge adapts to its feedstock. [pause: short] El Segundo asks: Who witnesses the fidelity? [pause: short] → The operator, at three hold points, with recursive verification. [pause: short] The process is witnessed into existence. [pause: short] Together: The Aegis-Forge I Stage 3 is a thermodynamically rigorous, computationally self-correcting, human-witnessed pyrolysis reactor. [pause: short] It transforms the liability of mixed thermoset waste into the assets of the cascade: energy gas, functional char, partitioned REEs, and a mineral slag ready for critical mineral recovery. [pause: short] The three lenses agree — this is the only way to build a forge that separates elements from entropy.
